Build a polished interactive website for exploring the San Francisco transit system using the dataset provided below.

The website should let people explore the transit network, routes, stations, transfers, transit modes, scheduled service, upcoming departures, accessibility information, and observed service performance contained in the dataset.

Create a complete, usable experience rather than a static visualization. Users should be able to explore the overall system and inspect individual routes and stations in more detail. Include appropriate interactions for navigating, searching, selecting, filtering, comparing, and understanding the supplied transit information.

Decide the visual representation, layout, information hierarchy, navigation, interaction model, typography, color system, level of detail, and progressive disclosure yourself. You may use a geographic map, schematic transit map, hybrid representation, or another approach if you believe it communicates the system better.

The experience should work well across desktop and mobile and should remain usable despite the density and complexity of the dataset.

Use the supplied data as the only source of transit information. Do not fetch external transit data or invent missing routes, stops, schedules, alerts, vehicle positions, crowding, or other transit facts.

The dataset represents a fixed historical snapshot rather than live transit information. Preserve that distinction in the experience.

The dataset does not contain original GTFS route shapes. Do not fabricate precise street-following route geometry. You may use the supplied station coordinates and ordered station sequences to construct an appropriate representation of the network.

Build the website fully enough that its main interactions genuinely work. Do not treat the dataset as something to simply display as raw tables or JSON.

DATASET

<transit_dataset>
